# WDD mapping sprint (2026-09-19, vast.ai A100)
Box layout: /workspace/wdd/{scripts,cache,results,logs}; results/FINDINGS.log one line per experiment; agg.py prints tables.
Caches: gpt2, smollm2, qwen05, pythia410 (=step143000), olmo1b (0724), gpt2_rand (random init), pythia410_step{0,1000,4000,16000,32000,64000}; 16,384 wikitext-test states, 64 train seqs for centering. (pile caches gpt2_pile/smollm2_pile queued)
Gate: new pipeline reproduces the paper's GPT-2 L6 numbers within 0.01.

## Findings so far
- e05 (4 models): identifiability is a NEURON property: ICC 0.95-0.97 everywhere. GPT-2: 32% always / 10% never; predictor = atom coherence (AUC 0.79), raw survival useless (0.45). Pythia/Qwen: raw survival AUC 0.81/0.77, coherence ~chance. SmolLM2: firing freq 0.75, survival 0.65. Two regimes: geometric (GPT-2) vs state (Pythia/Qwen).
- Paper's survival = CENTERED-state survival, clipped [-2,3]. Raw (physical) survival is a different quantity. (e08 will split.)
- e01 gpt2 layers: FVU32 0.16 (L0) -> 0.35 (L7-10) -> 0.19 (L11; sink gone, last block cancels the massive channel: sink share 0.00 at L0/L11, 0.90 at L2). Recall 0.85 L0 -> 0.63 L6 -> 0.56 L7 -> 0.32 L8 -> 0.21 L10 (collapse after L7; e22 will split by birth block). Token-embedding atom recall 0.69 at L0 -> 0.03 from L5, although its raw survival stays >1 (1.2-1.75) all the way: the token identity is reinforced, never erased, but unidentifiable by OMP (explained away). Bias atoms never selected (0.0).
- e01 gpt2_rand (random init): weight FVU == rotated (0.45-0.50) at all levels: zero alignment; recall 0.21 at L0 -> 0.01 by L4; embedding atom recall 1.00 at L0 decaying slowly. => alignment AND identifiability are learned.
- e03 dictionary: mutual coherence ~1 (twin cloud), Babel(64) 50-63 => coherence guarantees vacuous. Frame/second-order alignment cosF(S,Sigma) does NOT explain the rotation result (random dict scores higher). The matched-filter extreme statistic does: E max|cos(x,a)| weight/rot = 0.347/0.156 (gpt2), 0.389/0.178 (smollm2), 0.267/0.142 (pythia) ~ 2x. Tropp ERC<1 for the true top-3 support: 72% gpt2 (AUC for hit 0.69), 95% smollm2 (0.53), 98% pythia (0.53) => geometry limits GPT-2 only.
- qwen05 e01 partial: recall 0.87 at L5, 0.82 at L6 (mid-layer paper number 0.46 is a depth effect); token embedding survival 0.23-0.28 (erased, unlike GPT-2).
- e01 smollm2 (30 blocks): recall 0.98 L0, 0.94 through L8, 0.76 L12, 0.62 L18-23, 0.50 L27, 0.21 L28 (dip), 0.44 L29. Weight/rot FVU32 0.17/0.50 -> 0.30/0.40 (rotation floor DROPS with depth: 0.50 -> 0.39; states get lower-rank). Token-embedding raw survival explodes in the last layers (4.5, 8.1, 32 at L27-29; tied embeddings).
- e01 pythia410 partial: mid-late levels recall 0.25-0.28 but one-shot 0.5-0.6 (explaining-away is severe in Pythia).
- e02 pythia410 (delta-WDD, block increments over the block's own atoms): recall@8 0.55-0.75 (some blocks 0.87@32) vs global@64 0.42-0.48; rel err 0.15-0.24 (vs ~0.4 global); attention share of increment energy 0.1-0.25. Bad block b17 (0.28) is bad locally too.
- e03 olmo1b: ERC<1 98%, AUC 0.61; maxcorr weight 0.274 vs rot 0.102 (2.7x).
- Traceback note: Python prints the CURRENT source line in tracebacks, so a patched file makes an old process's traceback look like new code failed.
- e04 solvers (mid layer, |support|=64): OMP is the WORST identifier in all 4 models but the best reconstructor. GPT-2: OMP r1 0.64 / r3 0.12, one-shot 0.77, dual-frame one-shot (S^-1, Duffin-Schaeffer 1952) 0.80, CoSaMP 0.72 (best rel err 0.26), LASSO 0.85, NN-LASSO 0.86 (but LASSO coefs shrunk: ratio 0.35; refit FVU 0.43 vs OMP 0.18). Pythia: 0.47 -> dual 0.67, lasso 0.63-0.65. Qwen: 0.46 -> Sigma-whitened 0.64, dual 0.62, lasso 0.62. OLMo: 0.71 -> dual 0.83, Sigma 0.82, lasso 0.82. Nonnegativity: helps GELU (gpt2 +4pts), hurts SwiGLU (qwen -15, olmo -28; NN-lasso -35/-47) = the prior must match the nonlinearity. Identification ceiling is substantially a SOLVER ceiling (explaining-away).
- e08 cancellation anatomy: prominence |x_c.d|/|x_c| predicts one-shot identification at AUC 0.96-0.995 (one-shot = prominence reading); OMP AUC 0.84-0.92. Raw vs centered survival: GPT-2 raw 0.43 / centered 0.70 / DC term 0.68 (GPT-2's 'erasure' is mean subtraction: frequent writers); SmolLM2 0.63/0.85; Qwen 0.76/0.83; OLMo 0.77/0.81; Pythia 0.81/0.81 (no centering effect). Who erases raw-erased writes (mean fraction of the write): GPT-2 same-block other MLP neurons -0.81 (within-block cancellation!); SmolLM2 earlier MLP -0.87, later MLP -0.48 (the write opposes existing content); Pythia later MLP -0.74, later attn -0.31 (true downstream cleanup). Raw-erased writes are only 1-6% of tokens. DC atom / no centering: GPT-2 raw-only recall 0.665 > centered 0.635; Pythia raw 0.42 < centered 0.47. Centered-erased-but-raw-intact writes (4% of tokens) are unrecoverable in any frame (recall ~0.01).
- e06 gpt2: block-level writes REINFORCE (||x||^2 / sum ||block writes||^2 = 1.4 -> 2.8 at L10; 1.57 L11); oracle: the 64 largest TRUE writes (MLP+heads) explain the L6 state at FVU 0.62 (sum) / 0.405 (LS refit) vs OMP 0.179: more than half of OMP's explained variance is not attributable to the top-64 true writes. SmolLM2: token-embedding survival goes NEGATIVE with depth (-0.07 at L2 -> -1.4 at L17): tied embeddings, the model pushes away from the current token.
- Training (Pythia-410m mid layer): step0 FVU 0.583 = rot 0.586, recall 0.003; step1000 0.492/0.588 (alignment present within 1k steps), recall 0.03; step4000 0.475 (min), recall 0.16; 16k 0.41; 32k 0.49; 64k 0.52; 143k 0.475 (one-shot 0.58). Rel err 2.2 -> 4.7 -> 2.0 -> 1.1 -> 0.77 -> 0.51 -> 0.32: calibration keeps improving long after identification saturates.
- e07 gauge (SmolLM2): exact invariance (atoms match 1.000, OMP support identical 1.000); weight-norm ranking Spearman 0.08 under the gauge.
- e01 pythia410: recall 0.96 L0 -> 0.47 L12 -> 0.25 L14-17 -> 0.24 L20; olmo1b: 1.00 -> 0.67 (L9-12) -> 0.59 L15. Rotation floors differ by model (SmolLM2 0.39-0.50, GPT-2 0.51-0.55, Pythia 0.58-0.63, OLMo 0.76-0.80) = state isotropy.
- Pythia (neox) caches were built without the MLP output bias (dense_4h_to_h.bias); fixed in wdd_common + fix_neox_bias.py; only exact-accounting experiments (e06/e08 identity check 10%) were affected, not OMP.
- e01 all models: identification collapses in the last blocks everywhere (GPT-2 L8+ 0.32->0.21; Pythia L14+ 0.25, L21-23 0.10/0.08/0.01; SmolLM2 L28 0.21; OLMo L15 0.59) and the massive channel/sink is cancelled by the LAST block in all five (sink share 0.00 at the final level). Pythia support is ~40% token atoms at all levels. SmolLM2's last two blocks are 95% MLP energy with cancel index 0.30-0.36 (heavy mutual cancellation) and token-emb projection 8x/32x.
- e06 oracle (gpt2 L6 / smollm2 L15): summing the k=8 largest TRUE writes gives FVU 1.41 / 1.53 (> 1: the largest writes are mutually cancelling pairs); refit 0.82/0.80; OMP 0.60/0.54. At k=64: 0.62/0.83 sum, 0.41/0.48 refit, OMP 0.18/0.11. => the state is not sparse in its largest writes; ledger dominance != state prominence (e47 tests targets).
- e08 typical dominant write, median contributions to raw survival (raw = 1 + sum): GPT-2 raw 2.36 (same-block attn +0.33, later MLP +0.34, later attn +0.20, same-block other MLP -0.12; DC 0.69 -> centered 1.66); SmolLM2 raw 2.35 (earlier MLP +0.19, same-block MLP +0.19, later attn +0.14; DC 0.81); Pythia raw 1.18 (earlier attn +0.41, earlier MLP +0.27, later MLP -1.10!); Qwen raw 0.83 (earlier MLP +0.27, later MLP -0.56); OLMo raw 0.83 (later MLP -0.44). The eraser is always later MLPs, never attention; GPT-2/SmolLM2 reinforce, Pythia/Qwen/OLMo cancel. Explains e11: removing attention from Pythia states drops recall 0.47 -> 0.25 (attention had built +0.41 of the write).
- e11 v1 (gpt2): MLP-only synthetic states recall 0.612 (paper 0.62) but estimation error 0.24 vs 0.45: attention writes inflate coefficient estimates. Uncentered variants were an artifact (v2 queued).
- e12 shrinkage (held-out halves): GPT-2 median rel err 0.448 -> global shrink s=0.64: 0.181; log-linear (2 params): 0.122; per-decile: 0.120; ridge 1.0 refit (data-free): 0.251; pruning to top-8 atoms HURTS (0.61); oracle top-3 refit 0.58. SmolLM2 0.511 -> 0.339 / 0.200 / 0.200 / ridge1 0.287. => the estimation error is mostly systematic bias; caveat: the identified set is dominated by a few frequent neurons, so a global calibration partly predicts 'the neuron's typical coefficient'.
- e11 v1 qwen05: mlp_only 0.464 = baseline 0.461; minus_att 0.43.
- e09 residual-of-residual: after weight-OMP k=64, the residual is explained at k=32 by weight 0.561 / rot 0.529 / random 0.500 (gpt2), 0.450/0.422/0.383 (smollm2), 0.623/0.592/0.587 (pythia): alignment is fully exhausted (weight is even slightly worse on its own residual). The weight-vs-rotated FVU gap peaks at k=8 (0.24 gpt2, 0.26 smollm2, 0.11 pythia) and shrinks to 0.07-0.11 at k=64: the model's coordinate system shows in the first ~8 atoms; the tail is generic compression.
- e12 rest: Qwen 0.49 -> loglinear 0.21 (slope b=-0.16: the estimate carries almost no magnitude information; identified magnitudes cluster), Pythia 0.32 -> 0.16, OLMo 0.41 -> 0.20. Ridge 1.0 hurts OLMo (0.64). Calibration wins must be read with the narrow-magnitude caveat.
- e10 phase map (true atom always kept): recall rises as block atoms are removed: GPT-2 full (m/d 102) 0.64 -> 1/16 of block atoms 0.81; SmolLM2 0.68 -> 0.80; Pythia 0.47 -> 0.60; Qwen 0.46 -> 0.60. k barely matters (k16 vs k64 within 2-3 pts): the dominant write is picked early or never. Identification is governed by competition (m), not budget (k). FVU degrades only mildly with fewer atoms (gpt2 0.18 -> 0.24 at k64).
- e13 heads (5 models): WDD does not read attention. Dominant head's subspace selected in 2% (Qwen), 11% (SmolLM2), 19% (Pythia), 20% (GPT-2), 24% (OLMo) of tokens; energy captured when selected 0.01-0.19; head writes are not low-rank in the W_O SVD basis (top-16 of 64 directions carry 0.49-0.71 of write energy); attention atoms = 9-21% of the support but cos(recon_att, true_att) 0.09-0.23; true attention share of state energy 0.84-2.77 (cancelling).
- Training (Pythia step0): recall 0.003, ICC 0.68, 98% never; block-write cancel index 1.00 at all levels (orthogonal writes at init); trained GPT-2 1.4-2.8 (reinforcing): coherent block structure is learned.
- e09 olmo: alignment gap persists to k=64 (0.18) unlike the others (0.06-0.11).
- e53 pilot (gpt2, blocks 0-1, N=256): OV value-atom reconstruction of head writes is exact (err 0.000); attention mass on {t,t-1,t-2,0} median only 0.12-0.18; NNLS over the value atoms recovers the attention pattern from the head's WRITE alone at median corr 0.79-0.83 (an OV inverse, no q/k used). Full vectorized run launched (e53_ov_gpt2).
- e10 olmo: 0.71 -> 0.81 with 1/16 block atoms (k64); Pythia training step1000: recall 0.03, ICC 0.90, never 0.85.
- e53 OV (gpt2, N=128 tokens, blocks 0-6): value-atom reconstruction exact; attention mass on {t,t-1,t-2,0} 0.13-0.19 in blocks 0-1, 0.59-0.76 in blocks 2-6 with the SINK carrying 0.29-0.56 in blocks 3-6; attention pattern recovered from the head's write alone by NNLS over value atoms: median corr 0.83/0.79/0.60/0.68/0.67/0.85/0.52 (b0..b6). WDD + dominant head's value atoms: head selected 0.11 -> 0.27, and when selected 77% of the head's write energy is captured (static SVD atoms: 1%). Interpretation: mid-block attention writes are largely a per-head constant (sink value vector) that centering removes; the variable part is a dense mix of value atoms.
- Training (Pythia, e02/e05/e06): step0 ICC 0.68 / never 0.98; step1000 delta-recall@8 0.3-0.5 while global recall ~0.03 at depth (local structure precedes stream structure); step4000 delta 0.3-0.75; ICC 0.92-0.94 from step 4000 on; mid-stack cancel index falls with training (0.98 at 4k -> 0.57 at 64k) while early levels reinforce (1.2-1.5); token-embedding survival at L0 peaks mid-training (5x at 16k) then relaxes.
- e25 ledger sparsity (effective number of active MLP writes = participation ratio of c^2; top-64 energy share) at the mid layer: SmolLM2 156 (0.30), GPT-2 369 (0.29), Qwen 387 (0.23), OLMo 800 (0.17), Pythia 2214 (0.11); block-0 increments: SmolLM2 5, OLMo 7, Qwen 9, GPT-2 38, Pythia 200. Random-init GPT-2: 423 -> 5047 (every neuron alike, top-64 0.05); Pythia step1000 still dense (443-4778). Training sparsifies the ledger 10-20x.
- e02 delta-WDD (block increment over the block's own atoms, k=8 / k=32, vs global full-state k=64): GPT-2 0.27-0.75 / 0.30-0.80 vs 0.18-0.85 (better everywhere but block 0; blocks 2-3 and 8 hard even locally = within-block cancellation); SmolLM2 0.73-0.92 / 0.83-0.97 vs 0.30-0.66; Qwen 0.64-0.96 / 0.70-1.00 vs 0.15-0.92 (block 11: 0.64 vs 0.15); Pythia 0.24-0.96 / 0.43-0.99 vs 0.19-0.96. Delta-WDD reads gated models at 2-4x the identification rate of the full-state reading, with 8 atoms instead of 64.
- TRAINING TABLE (Pythia-410m, mid layer L12): step0 FVU 0.583=rot 0.586, recall 0.003, cancel 0.97, ICC 0.68, never 0.98 | step1000 0.492/0.588 (alignment), recall 0.03 (one-shot 0.06), cancel 1.22, PR 4778, delta-recall@8 0.35, ICC 0.90 | step4000 0.475, recall 0.16/0.29, cancel 0.83, PR 2587, ICC 0.92 | 16k 0.534, 0.41/0.57, cancel 0.69, PR 2015, ICC 0.94, always 0.05 | 32k 0.49/0.56, PR 1802 | 64k 0.52/0.61, cancel 0.57, PR 1710 | 143k 0.475/0.58, PR 2214, ICC 0.95, always 0.18 never 0.13. Rel err 2.2 -> 4.7 -> 2.0 -> 1.1 -> 0.77 -> 0.51 -> 0.32. Order of emergence: alignment (1k) -> block-local identifiability (1k-4k) -> global identifiability (4k-32k) -> calibration (through 143k); the neuron-property (ICC >= 0.9) is there from the first identifiable writes.
- e01 qwen05: recall 0.99 (L0-1) -> 0.46 (L12) -> 0.41 (L14); monotone decay with depth in every model.
- e56/e57 THE PROMINENCE LAW: identification is a step function of the dominant write's prominence |x_c.d|/|x_c|: <0.1: 0.02-0.10; 0.1-0.2: 0.33 (qwen) 0.44 (pythia) 0.72 (olmo); 0.2-0.3: 0.82-0.92; >=0.3: ~1.00. 50% crossing (one-shot k=64) GPT-2 0.19, SmolLM2 0.18 vs Gaussian extreme-value floor sqrt(2 ln m/d) 0.17 / 0.20 (measured random-z floor 0.156 / 0.180); k=16 crossing 0.24. Fraction of dominant writes above the floor 0.84 / 0.67 vs recall 0.77 / 0.68. Real states' strongest competitor correlation is ~2x the random floor (0.33 / 0.38 = structured competition). => a write is readable iff its prominence in the centered state beats the dictionary's noise floor; model differences in recall are differences in the prominence distribution (median 0.16 qwen/pythia, 0.18 olmo, 0.26-0.27 smollm2/gpt2).
- e15 gpt2 composition (read directions over write atoms): earlier 0.67-0.75 vs later 0.64-0.77 vs rotated 0.74-0.81: reads are only mildly sparse in writes and show no earlier/later asymmetry; WDD is about writes.
- e18 smollm2 noise: recall 0.69 (30 dB) -> 0.50 (0 dB) -> 0.41 (-3 dB); superposed foreign state at equal weight: own 0.53, foreign 0.54 (reads both).
- e16 smollm2 atoms-over-atoms: block-0 atoms are not sparse in other atoms (0.74 vs rot 0.78); last-block atoms are explained 95% by EARLIER atoms (late blocks re-write earlier directions); token embeddings are well explained by block atoms (0.48 vs 0.78), a third from block 0.
- e17 smollm2 diagnostics: recall by birth block: b0 0.95, b1 0.54, b2-5 0.12-0.22, b12 0.91, b14-15 0.82-0.88; by token frequency: unseen 0.46 -> 100-1000 count 0.87; block-0 atoms take 20% of the support for 7% of the energy.
- S1 synthetic (d=256, N=512, k=64; random unit dictionary): with NO cancellation, top-1 recall is 1.00 for every m/d (8-128), every K (4-256 writes per state), with or without coherent atom clusters, with either sign prior. A partner atom cancelling 40% of the write: 0.78-0.96; cancelling 80%: OMP 0.37-0.95, one-shot 0.2-0.7 (prominence 0.09-0.21). In synthetic states cancellation is the ONLY thing that breaks identification; OMP beats one-shot there because a single anti-aligned partner can be explained away (real models: the reverse, so real cancellation must be diffuse -> S1b).
- S2 first cell (p=0): state-level recall 0.92, increment-level 1.00 even with no cross-block coupling (dilution by 128 writes).
- e57 all five: 50% crossing of one-shot(k=64) identification vs sqrt(2 ln m/d): GPT-2 0.19/0.17, SmolLM2 0.18/0.20, Pythia 0.14/0.15, Qwen 0.14/0.17, OLMo 0.11/0.11; k=16 crossings 0.17-0.24 (fewer slots, higher bar). Fraction of dominant writes above the floor vs one-shot recall: 0.84/0.77, 0.67/0.68, 0.53/0.58, 0.50/0.61, 0.81/0.78. Real states' strongest competitor is 1.7-2.5x the random floor (structured competition) but the threshold still sits at the random floor.
- e20 gpt2 sinks: every sink state is one neuron's write: L1 mlp1#1120 (channel 138, 93% of energy, fvu1 0.022); L2 and L6 mlp2#1825 (channel 447, 92-93%, fvu1 0.06); the first atom is that neuron for 100% of sinks.
- S1b (flawed construction, keep as a lesson): cancelling a write 'diffusely' with random near-orthogonal atoms needs cancelling energy 8x the write, so the state is dominated by the cancellers (prominence 0.03, recall 0); real cancellers must be anti-aligned (moderate cosine) or non-atoms. Concentrated (one partner, cos -0.8) at f=0.8-1.1: OMP 0.9-0.99 vs one-shot 0.0-0.5: OMP explains a single partner away; real models show the reverse.
- e21 lifetime (gpt2, larger half of block-b0 dominant writes; recall / raw survival by level): born b1: L1 0.61/2.05 -> L6 0.35/2.79 -> L11 0.09/5.53; born b2: 0.49/2.1 -> 0.09/6.8; born b3: 0.34/2.8 -> 0.03/12.8. Identification decays with depth while the write's own direction is AMPLIFIED 3-13x: the direction becomes shared by later reinforcing writers, so attribution to the original writer is ambiguous (aliasing by reinforcement), not erasure.
- e20 smollm2 sinks: L2 sink = block-2 #90 (73%, ch507); L15 sink = block-11 #1229 (100%, ch507, fvu1 0.006): the sink channel is rebuilt by a later neuron.
- S1e (valid, appended partner atoms; f=0.8, K=64 writes, m=64d): CANCELLATION (survival 0.2, prominence 0.05-0.16, floor 0.27): one-shot 0.00-0.38 = prominence-limited; OMP peels cancellers when they are FEW atoms with sizeable coefficients (P=1 in-dict: 1.00 even at prominence 0.05, partner selected 100%; P=8 cos-0.2 in-dict 0.89 vs non-atom 0.01) but not when diffuse (P=32: 0.22-0.75 in-dict, 0.29-0.93 non-atom; partners selected <2%). REINFORCEMENT at f=0.8 (survival 1.8): 1.00 for every solver, P, cosine, in or out of the dictionary. => real models' one-shot > OMP and the depth decay need either heavier reinforcement (S1f) or true twins.
- S2 v2 (partial): p=0 state 0.93 / increment 1.00; p=0.25 reinforce-coupling: state 0.73 / increment 1.00.
- S1f heavy reinforcement (later writers add 2-4x the original along its direction): a SINGLE near-twin atom (cos 0.9) with the larger coefficient: OMP recall of the original 0.44 (f=2) / 0.36 (f=4) while one-shot = 1.00 (partner selected 100%: OMP takes the twin and explains the original away) = the real regime (one-shot > OMP); cos 0.5 or 8 smaller reinforcers: 1.00 everywhere. Non-atom reinforcers: 1.00. => the one-shot advantage is the signature of dominant near-twin true writers; the depth decay of e21 is 'a bigger twin took the direction'.
- e17 olmo diag: recall by birth block b0 0.71, b1 0.13 (the super-weight block), b2 0.34, b3 0.48, b5-8 0.90-0.99; very frequent tokens 0.38 vs rare 0.81.
- S2 v2: p=0.25 cancel-coupling: state 0.68 / increment 1.00.
- e22 birth matrix (gpt2; recall / share of tokens whose dominant write was born in block b): block-0 writes are the dominant write for 100% (L0) -> 56% (L6) -> 16% (L8) of tokens and are identified at 0.72-0.85 at every level; block-1: 0.5-0.7; block 2-3: 0.3-0.5; from L8 on the dominant write is late-born (b8: 73% of tokens at L8, b9: 76% at L9, b11: 70% at L11) and those are identified at 0.18-0.31 (block-4/5/6-born writes read at 0.7-0.9 even at L8). The late collapse is 'the largest write is a member of a within-block cancelling pair' (e08: same-block MLP -0.81), i.e. the target problem, not erasure of old writes.
- e06 cancel index at the mid layer: GPT-2 1.86, SmolLM2 1.34 (reinforcing) vs Qwen 0.58, Pythia 0.39, OLMo 0.33 (cancelling); the same split as raw survival (2.4 vs 0.8-1.2), prominence (0.26 vs 0.16-0.18) and recall.
## Claims under test (structure for the final synthesis; each = hypothesis with evidence for/against)
1. WDD's computation is classical sparse approximation over a fixed dictionary (OMP 1993 / BP 1998): supported (e04/e45 solvers; S1 phase behaviour; e10 competition-not-budget).
2. Identification is governed by prominence vs the dictionary's extreme-value floor sqrt(2 ln m/d): e56/e57 crossings within 0.03 in 5 models; e58 = out-of-sample test across all levels + checkpoints (pending).
3. Provenance (the label) is the novel ingredient; reconstruction cannot certify it: paper's provenance null + e09 residual + S2(B) non-uniqueness (pending) + e13 attention.
4. Gauge invariance is exact (e07) and is what weight-magnitude methods lack.
5. Residual-stream-specific twist = cancellation (raw-erased 1-6%, diffuse, by later MLPs) and REINFORCEMENT by near-twins (S1f: one-shot > OMP; e21/e22 depth decay); the two regimes: reinforcing (GPT-2, SmolLM2) vs cancelling (Pythia, Qwen, OLMo) models.
6. Attention is not readable with static atoms (e13); OV value atoms read 77% of a head's write (e53); DC/sink part is centered out.
7. Identifiability is a neuron property (ICC 0.95+) and may be UNRELATED to causal importance (e59 pending: can WDD be fooled?).
- e58 law test (gpt2, 12 levels, no fitting): predicted = P(prominence > sqrt(2 ln m_L/d)) vs measured one-shot recall: corr 0.93, MAE 0.11, bias +0.11 (floor too low: real competitors ~2x the random floor); vs OMP corr 0.98. Captures the L8 collapse (pred 0.49 / measured 0.43).
- e58 smollm2 (30 levels, no fitting): corr 0.99, MAE 0.034, bias -0.03 vs one-shot; vs OMP corr 0.99 MAE 0.026. The law predicts the whole depth profile (0.99 -> 0.55).
- e21 smollm2 lifetime: block-1 writes 0.88 -> 0.00 identification (L1 -> L29) while raw survival 2.3 -> 4.2 (amplified, not erased), as in GPT-2.
- e58 LAW TEST, all five (no fitting; predicted one-shot recall = P(prominence > sqrt(2 ln m_L/d)) per level): GPT-2 12 levels corr 0.93 MAE 0.11 (bias +0.11); SmolLM2 30 levels 0.99 / 0.034; Qwen 24 levels 0.97 / 0.057; Pythia 24 levels 0.92 / 0.060 (bias 0.00; vs OMP only 0.73 = Pythia's explaining-away gap); OLMo 16 levels 0.99 / 0.021. 106 (model, level) points; the final-level collapses are predicted (Pythia L23 0.04/0.02). The prominence law governs the one-shot (projection) reading; OMP deviates where near-twin competition adds explaining-away (Pythia, GPT-2).
- e22 smollm2 birth matrix: writes from every block identified at 0.8-0.98 through L7; block-0 writes remain the dominant write for 68-92% of tokens up to L7.
- e58 training: step4000 corr 0.99 bias -0.115; 16k 0.97 / -0.095; 64k 0.98 / -0.04; 143k 0.92 / 0.00: the bias vanishes with training (competition rises to the random floor as the model learns).
- e59 CAN WDD BE FOOLED (gpt2, single-neuron ablation dCE on 16 held-out seqs): always-identified neurons (n=40): median 0.0003, mean 0.0033, q90 0.0046; never-identified (n=18): median 0.0007, mean 0.0085, q90 0.033; middle 0.0006/0.0034; block-matched random controls ~0.0000-0.0001. => readable != important; the never-set is 2.6x (mean) / 7x (q90) more costly to remove. Prominence (reinforced, redundant) anti-tracks importance, in line with the paper's R7.
- e23 context copies (5 models): selected token-embedding atoms (9-36% of the support) match the current token 0.0-2.7% (null 0.7-0.9%), the previous token 0.0%, any earlier context token 0.2-1.5% vs null 33-37%: NOT attention copies; anti-context fillers (the anomalous-embedding cloud).
- e22 pythia / qwen birth matrices: block-0 writes identified 0.90-0.99 at every level; the poorly read classes are block 2 (pythia 0.15-0.6) and blocks 1-2 (qwen 0.2-0.55) = the super-weight/massive-channel blocks.
- e59 smollm2: always 0.0017/0.0094 (median/mean) vs never 0.0013/0.0128: no relation between readability and importance.
- e24 transfer (gpt2): per-neuron recall WikiText vs Pile (82 neurons with >=20 dominant tokens in both): Spearman 0.91, Pearson 0.96, mean |diff| 0.06; always-agree 0.81, never-agree 0.80; corpus recall 0.641 vs 0.622. Identifiability is a stable per-neuron certificate across corpora.
- S2 v2 layered synthetic stream (8 blocks x 16 writes, coupling p = probability that a block's dominant write goes along a new atom near-collinear (cos 0.9) with an earlier dominant write): state-level recall 0.93 (p=0) -> 0.73/0.68/0.69 (p=.25 reinforce/cancel/mixed) -> 0.55/0.50/0.51 (p=.5) -> 0.40/0.36/0.35 (p=.75) -> 0.29 (p=1 reinforce); increment-level recall 1.00 at every p. Cross-block direction sharing is what degrades state-level attribution; the increment reading is immune. Matches e02 (delta-WDD gains largest where sharing is highest).
- e59b rank (gpt2, 132 neurons ablated): Spearman(dCE, identification rate) -0.23; vs prominence -0.09; vs raw survival +0.07; vs dominance count +0.42; mean dCE by identification tercile low/mid/high 0.0084 / 0.0001 / 0.0030 (least readable third costs 2.8x the most readable third).
- e21 pythia lifetime: block-1 writes recall 0.77 -> 0.00 with raw survival 2.0 -> 0.57 (true erasure by later MLPs), vs GPT-2/SmolLM2 where survival GROWS; two mechanisms (erasure vs shared-direction amplification), one observable.
- e00 gpt2_pile: FVU32 0.347 (rot 0.529), recall 0.621, one-shot 0.720 (wikitext 0.640 / 0.765): replicates.
- e24 smollm2 transfer: Spearman 0.77 Pearson 0.98, always-agree 1.00, never-agree 0.86. e59 pythia: all ablation costs ~0.0001-0.002 in both groups (mid-layer neurons individually negligible; no relation). e22 olmo: block-1 writes 0.98 at L1 -> 0.21 at L7 (the super-weight block's writes become unreadable), other blocks 0.6-1.0.
- e40 vocab (gpt2 mid layer): unembedding projections of always- vs never-identified atoms are equally flat (kurtosis 0.4 vs 0.3, top-10 concentration 0.001); never-set top tokens are function words/punctuation 44% vs 18%: no vocabulary-space signature of readability at the mid layer.
- e59/e59b importance across models: Spearman(dCE, identification) GPT-2 -0.23, SmolLM2 +0.09; group means always vs never: GPT-2 0.0033 vs 0.0085, SmolLM2 0.0094 vs 0.0128, Qwen 0.0025 vs 0.0008, Pythia 0.0001 vs -0.0004 (negligible). => readability is ORTHOGONAL to single-neuron causal importance (|rho| <= 0.25, sign varies). Dominance count correlates with importance (+0.4) in both models tested.
- S2(B) non-uniqueness counterexample (m=32d, 1000 dense writers whose exact sum lies in the span of 10 other atoms): OMP k=64 reconstructs at FVU 0.000, selects all 10 non-writers (100%), real writers are 10% of the support. Provenance is not a property of a state when m > d; reconstruction cannot certify it (same lesson as the paper's provenance null and e09, now as a hard construction).
- e60 INTERVENTION (gpt2, only the dominant write's own projection is changed): erased writes (survival < 0.5, n=75): +0c 0.04/0.04 (omp/one-shot; prom 0.09, 7% above floor) -> +0.25c 0.12/0.09 -> +0.5c 0.23/0.21 -> +1.0c 0.47/0.39 (prom 0.19, 59% above floor). Intact writes (n=3929): -0c 0.65/0.79 (prom 0.28, 87% above) -> -0.5c 0.34/0.51 (64%) -> -0.9c 0.12/0.25 (36%). Identification follows the write's own prominence causally, in both directions, along the same curve as the cross-level law.
- e41 aliasing (5 models): fraction of misses whose max-coherence partner atom (data-free) is in the support: GPT-2 0.22 (null 0.002), SmolLM2 0.15, Qwen 0.11, Pythia 0.09, OLMo 0.17; top-3 partners 0.43/0.25/0.22/0.19/0.33; alias coefficient has the right sign 0.89-1.00 and is 1.7-3.5x c*cos; partners are MLP atoms 77-99%, same block 16-53%. Recall with alias credit +0.05-0.08 (GPT-2 0.641 -> 0.719).
- e21 qwen lifetime: block-1 writes 1.00 -> 0.00 with survival 1.5 -> 2.2 (amplified), block-2 writes 0.97 -> 0.04 with survival 1.3 -> 0.55 (erased): both mechanisms inside one model.
- e60 smollm2 intervention: erased (n=148) +0c 0.20/0.47 (omp/os; above-floor 0.48) -> +0.5c 0.57/0.74 (0.72) -> +1.0c 0.89/0.89 (0.91); intact (n=3622) -0c 0.72/0.71 (0.70) -> -0.5c 0.43/0.42 (0.47) -> -0.9c 0.29/0.22 (0.32). One-shot recall = fraction above the floor at every dose, causally.
- e59b pythia rank: Spearman(dCE, recall) -0.10 (nothing; all costs ~0.0001-0.002).
- e42 dictionary ablations (gpt2 L6, k=64): full 78k atoms recall 0.635 / one-shot 0.762 / FVU32 0.338; no embeddings (27k) 0.651 / 0.765 / 0.349; no attention atoms 0.641 / 0.782 / 0.348; MLP only (21.5k) 0.656 / 0.785 / 0.364; MLP+emb 0.641; twins merged 0.633. Position atom recall 0.000. Embedding and attention atoms contribute ~1 pt of FVU and cost ~2 pts of recall: both competition and reconstruction live among the MLP atoms.
- e43 PCA per level (gpt2, out-of-sample PCA at k=32): PCA 0.35 (L0) -> 0.61 (L7-9) -> 0.25 (L11) vs WDD 0.16 -> 0.35 -> 0.17: the dictionary beats PCA by 0.2-0.26 at every level; state effective rank 71 -> 278 (L8) -> 40 (L11): the final-level FVU drop is the state becoming low-rank.
- e60 qwen intervention: erased (n=902) 0.11/0.17 -> +0.25c 0.53/0.54 -> +0.5c 0.80/0.75 -> +1.0c 0.96/0.91 (above-floor 0.07 -> 0.89); intact (n=1837) 0.67/0.83 -> -0.5c 0.37/0.56 -> -0.9c 0.28/0.39 (0.77 -> 0.34). Causal in Qwen.
- e59 olmo: always 0.0005/0.0017 (median/mean) vs never 0.0007/0.0038: unrelated (never ~2x mean). Final tally: readability orthogonal to single-neuron importance in all five.
- e61 null-space (clarification, not a result as posed): the min-norm / dual-frame linear inverse c_min = A(A^T A)^-1 x spreads the coefficients over all atoms (top-1 ratio 0.01-0.05) so 97-98% of the sparse ledger's coefficient energy is 'in the null space' trivially; it keeps the SIGN of the top-3 writes (0.89-0.98). The meaningful identifiability statement is the sparse one: uniqueness of the top-3 support is governed by ERC (e03: <1 for 72-98% of tokens), and what a model hides from any state reading is the cancelling-pair content (S2B; oracle FVU > 1). Increment-level min-norm readings are less spread (block 0: 0.43-0.83 vs 0.97 at state level).
- e30 injection (partial, dose 0.1 x state norm, killed for time): the atom of a never-identified neuron injected into random typical states is recovered at 0.14 (gpt2) / 0.12 (smollm2) vs 0.30 / 0.28 for always-identified neurons and 0.24 / 0.28 for middle ones, at identical dose and recovered coefficient ratio ~0.96-1.0: the never-class is partly geometric (its direction competes worse in the dictionary), not only a matter of natural magnitude or survival.
- e60 pythia intervention: erased (n=447) 0.02/0.03 -> +1.0c 0.66/0.56 (above-floor 0.02 -> 0.55); intact (n=2839) 0.62/0.75 -> -0.9c 0.09/0.12 (0.70 -> 0.09). Causal in 4 of 4 tested so far.
- e43 smollm2: WDD beats out-of-sample PCA by ~0.2 FVU at every level; effective rank 119 (L0) -> 33-56 (L2-7) -> 152 (L10) -> ~100: SmolLM2's early-middle states are low-rank.
- e02 smollm2_pile: delta-WDD block-local 0.71-0.99 (k8) vs global 0.28-0.98: replicates on the Pile. e59b qwen rank +0.10.
- e44 PIPELINE at matched budget (64 atoms; global OMP on the state vs per-block OMP on the increments (hidden states only) + the token atom, refit on the full state): GPT-2 top-1 0.635 -> 0.788, top-3 0.12 -> 0.35, real share of selected MLP atoms 0.34 -> 0.74-0.79, spurious coefficient mass 0.57 -> 0.17-0.21, rel err 0.45 -> 0.21-0.30, full-state FVU 0.18 -> 0.45. SmolLM2 top-1 0.688 -> 0.999, top-3 0.12 -> 0.97, real share 0.24 -> 0.84, spurious 0.67 -> 0.15, FVU 0.11 -> 0.44. Attribution and reconstruction trade off; WDD is a decomposition of writes, not of the state.
- e42 pythia/qwen: MLP-only recall 0.500 / 0.489 vs full 0.472 / 0.461; twins merged: no change (pythia one-shot 0.657 vs 0.584 though).
- e60 olmo intervention: erased (n=690) 0.29/0.21 -> +1.0c 1.00/0.98; intact (n=1802) 0.86/0.97 -> -0.9c 0.25/0.22. CAUSAL IN 5/5 MODELS.
- e44 pipeline pythia: top-1 0.47 -> 0.87, top-3 0.05 -> 0.48, real share 0.29 -> 0.77, FVU 0.30 -> 0.73; qwen: 0.46 -> 0.98, 0.04 -> 0.90, 0.29 -> 0.81, FVU 0.22 -> 0.63. Universal.
- e45 solver round 2 (gpt2): dual-OMP 0.66 (selection whitening alone adds little to OMP); dual one-shot k16/32/64/128/256: r1 0.63/0.71/0.80/0.87/0.91, r3 0.17/0.26/0.37/0.49/0.58, FVU 0.70/0.63/0.53/0.39/0.23; LASSO+refit 0.85/0.40 err 0.28 FVU 0.43; screen-128-then-OMP 0.77. At k=256 the dual one-shot identifies 0.91 and reconstructs 0.23 (OMP 0.18): the trade-off can be bought with budget.
- e46 solver-vs-state (gpt2): recall omp 0.636 / dual one-shot 0.801 / one-shot 0.767 / lasso 0.845 / union 0.868; OMP-never neurons (n=7) under the other solvers 0.18-0.25; fraction of never-neurons omp 0.15 -> 0.09 for every other solver; ICC 0.95-0.96 for all. ~9% of dominant neurons are state-limited (no solver reads them), ~6% OMP-limited.
- e62 offset (mid layer): thresholds gauss / random-max / random-64th / real-competitor-max / real-64th: GPT-2 0.171/0.156/0.117/0.331/0.196, SmolLM2 0.198/0.180/0.134/0.376/0.202, Qwen 0.166/0.153/0.119/0.290/0.139. P(prom > real-64th) = 0.782/0.661/0.620 vs one-shot recall 0.765/0.679/0.613 (identity by construction for one-shot@64: the true atom is in the top-64 iff its correlation beats the 64th competitor). Substantive part: the real competitor level is 1.0-1.7x the random-dictionary 64th-order floor; c* = multiple of the Gaussian max-floor reproducing recall: one-shot 1.18/0.99/0.84, OMP 1.40/0.98/1.04. The Gaussian floor is a +-0.1 approximation; prominence is the operative variable (e60); recall differences between models are prominence-distribution differences.
- e46 smollm2: OMP-never neurons under dual 0.64 / lasso 0.58 / union 0.66; never fraction 0.16 -> 0.03: SmolLM2's never-class is solver-limited; GPT-2's (0.15 -> 0.09) is state-limited.
- e45 pythia: dual one-shot k=256 r1 0.78 r3 0.35 FVU 0.22 (OMP 0.47 / 0.04 / 0.30).
- e47 TARGETS (gpt2 / smollm2): ledger top-1: omp 0.64/0.68, dual 0.80/0.82, lasso 0.85/0.84; MOST PROMINENT true write (largest |x_c.d| among real writes): omp 0.75/0.67, dual 0.97/0.99, lasso 0.99/0.97; net-present (|c| x raw survival): 0.08-0.22 (a poor target: dominated by huge fractional survivors). Ledger top-1 = most prominent only 31% / 19% of tokens. WDD reads the most prominent true write near-perfectly; the 'ceiling' is largely the choice of target.
- e48 error bars (5 models): the OLS/Cramer-Rao standard error from the support does not predict the actual error (Spearman -0.35..+0.23; 2-sigma coverage 0.15-0.42; z median 0.1-4.3, MAD 2-7): error = misspecification bias from absorbed writes, not noise. Negative.
- e46 qwen: never-neurons stay never under every solver (0.03-0.08; never fraction 0.18 -> 0.14-0.18) = state-limited; pythia 0.11 -> 0.06 (partly solver). e45 olmo: dual k=256 r1 0.92 r3 0.65 FVU 0.37.
- e49 dual-frame depth sweep (gpt2): 0.86 (L0) -> 0.80 (L6) -> 0.41 (L8) -> 0.31 (L9-10), one-shot 0.87 -> 0.76 -> 0.44 -> 0.43; late-born share 0.78-0.92 and prominence 0.17 from L8: the late collapse is a state property.
- e47 all five, most-prominent-true-write target: omp 0.75/0.67/0.92/0.65/0.83, dual 0.97/0.99/0.98/0.94/0.99, lasso 0.99/0.97/1.00/0.73/0.96 (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia/olmo); ledger top-1 = most prominent in 18-31% of tokens (OLMo 23%).
- e49 dual-frame depth (all): identification decays with depth under the dual solver too, tracking the median prominence (e.g. Qwen 1.00 -> 0.58 as prominence 0.59 -> 0.16; OLMo 1.00 -> 0.73; Pythia 0.98 -> 0.50; SmolLM2 1.00 -> 0.9): state property, not solver.
- e41 late levels: at the last levels the max-coherence partners of the dominant writes are ATTENTION atoms (GPT-2 L11 69%, L9 74%; Pythia L21 86%; Qwen L23 58%) with partner cosines 0.54-0.94 (near-twins at Pythia L21 0.92, GPT-2 L11 0.94): late MLP write directions lie inside attention output subspaces; alias credit adds only +0.01-0.04 there.
- e51 recall-vs-k (smollm2/pythia/olmo): recall@1 0.17/0.13/0.18, @8 0.63/0.41/0.54, saturates by k=16-24 (0.67/0.45/0.64); when found, median pick position 1-2 (q90 6-15); first atom is MLP 96-99%, a real write 43-58%, the dominant write 13-18%; the first atom removes only 7-15% of state energy. (gpt2/qwen runs crashed on a CUDA index assert; dropped.)
- e52 WITHIN-NEURON estimation (caveat): within-neuron Pearson(c_hat, c) median gpt2 0.29, smollm2 0.05, qwen 0.18, pythia 0.38, olmo 0.70; between-neuron share of identified-coefficient variance 0.84/0.97/0.97/0.55/0.97; CV of true coefficients within a neuron 0.03-0.20. WDD reads WHICH neuron with its typical magnitude; token-level variation within a neuron is weakly recovered except in OLMo. The calibration wins (e12) largely encode per-neuron typical magnitudes.
- e54 attention DC: GPT-2 heads have a large constant part (median DC share 0.44, 42% of heads > 0.5; blocks 1-3 0.52-0.66) while SmolLM2/Qwen/Pythia/OLMo heads are mostly variable (median 0.06-0.12; Qwen block 0 0.64). The summed attention DC is 50-100x the norm of a centered state and is the mean (cos with mu 0.81-0.91 in GPT-2/SmolLM2/Qwen; 0.18/0.32 in Pythia/OLMo). Centering removes attention's constant part everywhere; the DC explanation of unreadable attention is GPT-2-specific.
- e25 pythia step0: PR 557 (L0) -> 10,400 (L18): dense at init.
- e114 increment-level law: SmolLM2 corr(pred, one-shot@8) 0.91, MAE 0.07 over 30 blocks; GPT-2 corr -0.43, predicted 0.60-0.96 vs measured 0.25-0.52 (block 0: 0.96 vs 0.27): the random-floor form of the law FAILS where a block's atoms are internally coherent (GPT-2); the bar is the real competitor level. Consistent with GPT-2's coherence-driven identification (e05 AUC 0.79).
- e116 (data-free): per block, an MLP atom's max-coherence partner is another MLP atom of an EARLIER block 30-38% of the time in GPT-2 blocks 1-11 (SmolLM2 11-30%), an attention atom 6-17%, a token atom 0-6%. ('energy in own attention span' is trivially 1.00: a block's head subspaces jointly span R^d; design flaw, ignore.)
- e117 block 0 as extended embedding: GPT-2 cos(MLP0 write, token emb) +0.10 (95% positive), |MLP0| = 7.9x |emb|, R2 of the MLP0 write from the token id alone 0.72; SmolLM2 cos -0.03, 15.8x, R2 1.00 (block-0 MLP is a pure token lookup).
- e106 increment residual (k=8): GPT-2 residual FVU 0.35-0.71 with attention 14-26% of it; MLP-only increments are not 8-sparse either (fvu8 0.43-0.68); SmolLM2 block 2: 0.11.
- e114 increment law (cont.): OLMo corr(pred, omp@8) 0.97 MAE 0.025 (block 1 = super-weight block is the outlier: pred 0.93 vs 0.28); Pythia corr 0.56 MAE 0.19. The random-floor law is exact where block dictionaries are incoherent (SmolLM2, OLMo) and fails where they are internally coherent (GPT-2, partly Pythia).
- e117 pythia: MLP0 write R2 from token id 1.00, |MLP0| = 14x |emb|; olmo 0.75, 3.6x. Block-0 MLPs are token lookups in SmolLM2/Pythia, context-dependent in GPT-2/OLMo (R2 0.72/0.75).
- e116 olmo block 0: 48% of MLP-0 atoms have a TOKEN atom as their nearest partner (token-lookup block); pythia block 0: 20%.
- e55 sink killer: GPT-2 block 11 removes 88% of the sink's channel-447 projection, attention 97% of the removal (MLP 3%), sink norm ratio 14 -> 1; SmolLM2 block 28 removes 82% of channel 507, MLP 100% (attention 0), ratio 29 -> 5. The sink is cancelled by different components in different models.
- e87 co-active geometry (gpt2/smollm2): a token's top-3 true writes are nearly orthogonal (|cos|<0.1 for 59%/66% of pairs; median signed cos +0.05; cancelling pairs <-0.3 only 3%/2%; reinforcing >0.3 18%/6%); top-1 vs the attention sum +0.14/+0.07. Dominant writes do not cancel each other: the crowd of small writes cancels them (diffuse cancellation).
- e89 mean: the centering mean is ~16-sparse in the dictionary (FVU 0.06/0.04 after 16 atoms): GPT-2 mlp1#1128 (-12), attention atoms, mlp6#578; SmolLM2 mlp2#90 (+67, the sink neuron), mlp1#557, attention atoms; no overlap with the top-10 dominant writers.
- e74 residual (gpt2/smollm2): the k=64 residual holds 0.00 of the dominant write and only 8%/7% attention, 10%/9% MLP by least squares on the token's own component vectors: the residual is not aligned with any block's write (noise-like).
- e101 co-selection vs co-firing (gpt2): corr 0.03 (different-block pairs 0.04); co-selection vs coherence 0.07: WDD support co-occurrence carries no circuit information. Negative.
- e11 v2 (re-centered, gpt2/smollm2): removing all attention writes (oracle) changes recall 0.635 -> 0.614 / 0.688 -> 0.672; MLP-only synthetic states 0.612 / 0.669; attention-only states 0.12 / 0.02; attention writes inflate the coefficient error (0.24 -> 0.45 in GPT-2). Attention is irrelevant to MLP identification in these two models; v1's Pythia drop awaits v2.
- e63 spark/conditioning: true top-3 supports are well conditioned everywhere (cond 1.2-1.4, smin 0.83-0.92); top-8: GPT-2 cond 3.4, ERC median 1.42 (<1 in 3%) vs SmolLM2 cond 1.5, ERC 0.97 (<1 in 57%); top-16 GPT-2 cond 5.9. Support uniqueness is never the issue at k=3; at k=8 GPT-2's coherence bites.
- e55 qwen: block 21 removes 97% of the sink (MLP 100%), ratio 31 -> 1; pythia: block 22 removes 53% (shares unreliable).
- e101 smollm2/qwen: co-selection vs co-firing 0.16 / 0.14 (weak); e74 qwen/pythia: residual holds 0.00 of the dominant write, attention 10-14%.
- BUG (fixed): token-atom keys (block -1) could exceed the ledger width when the token index > DFF (e102, e51 crashes on gpt2/qwen/smollm2); e44 used a safe where-mask and is unaffected.
- e102 fillers (pythia): token atoms are 33% of the support but the top-200 most-selected token atoms cover only 2% of token selections (selected tokens are diverse and rare: 90% unseen in the centering slice); spurious MLP selections = 71% of MLP selections spread over 52k distinct atoms (top-200 cover 10%); pruning the 400 most frequent fillers: recall 0.471 -> 0.495, FVU unchanged. The spurious support is diffuse, not a fixed filler set.
- e87 pythia: top-3 writes ~orthogonal (72% |cos|<0.1), cancelling pairs 1%. e89 pythia: mean 16-atom FVU 0.19 (less sparse than GPT-2/SmolLM2). e101 pythia: co-selection vs co-firing -0.03. e74 olmo: residual attention 21% MLP 23%.
- e11 v2 pythia: minus attention (re-centered) 0.274 vs baseline 0.472; mlp-only 0.271; mlp+att 0.461: confirmed, attention reinforces the dominant MLP writes' prominence in Pythia (e08: earlier attention +0.41); attention-only states 0.06.
- e102 gpt2: top-200 token fillers = 5% of token selections (40% unseen, embedding norm 3.4 vs 3.95 typical); spurious MLP 66% over 21k distinct atoms; pruning 400: recall 0.636 -> 0.615. No filler set in either model.
- e63 qwen: top-8 ERC median 0.82 (<1 in 82%), cond 1.4; e87 olmo: 80% of top-3 pairs |cos|<0.1; e89 olmo: mean is not sparse (16-atom FVU 0.28) and small (|mu| 1.2 vs |x| 3.4).
## AUDIT (independent code audit of the key scripts, 2026-09-19 ~04:30 UTC) and corrections
- CORRECTION e54: att_dc_total summed over 8192 rows; the true ratio of the summed attention DC to a centered state's norm is 0.64/0.62/0.72/0.52/1.19 (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia/olmo), not 50-100x. Qualitative point (centering removes attention's constant part; cos with mu 0.8-0.9 in three models) stands.
- CORRECTION e08 pythia: computed before the NeoX bias fix (identity check 0.10): the Pythia 'who erases' contributions are from an incomplete decomposition; re-run queued. AUCs/survival medians/DC atom unaffected.
- REFRAME prominence law: the one-shot statement is definitional (top-64 by correlation contains d iff |x_c.d| beats the 64th competitor); sqrt(2 ln m/d) is the expected MAX over random atoms (k=1 statistic), and the k=64 crossing matching it is a coincidence between the real 64th competitor (0.14-0.20) and the random max (0.11-0.20); within a model the floor is constant across levels, so e58's cross-level correlations (0.92-0.99) show that the PROMINENCE DISTRIBUTION explains identification across depth, models and training, not that the threshold equals the floor. Non-definitional residue: OMP tracks prominence (AUC 0.84-0.92; e58 vs OMP corr 0.73-0.99); the competitor level is level-independent and ~1.0-1.2x the random max floor (e62 c*); the token-level intervention (e60) is causal for OMP (for one-shot it follows by construction). Possible anisotropy: sqrt(2 ln m / d_eff) with d_eff ~150 gives ~0.35 = the real competitor max (0.33); e118 tests a covariance-matched floor.
- CAVEAT e47: 'most prominent true write' is the solver's own statistic restricted to true atoms (near-circular); e119 reports the target's ledger rank and a non-circular target (largest |c| among writes with raw survival > 0.75).
- CORRECTION e41: proper null = the partner's presence in the support of HITS: gpt2 misses 0.217 vs hits 0.098 (excess +0.12), smollm2 0.148 vs 0.114 (+0.03), qwen 0.110 vs 0.096 (+0.01), pythia 0.087 vs ?, olmo 0.174 vs ?; aliasing by the max-coherence partner is real in GPT-2 only (~+0.12), marginal elsewhere. 'Recall with alias credit' over-credits by the base rate.
- CAVEAT e44: in 'equal' mode SmolLM2/Qwen/Pythia used 49/53/53 atoms (not 64); recall/real-share conclusions robust (energy mode agrees), the FVU trade-off is overstated for those three (fewer atoms and still worse FVU is direction-consistent). e46 never-groups are n=4-7 (underpowered). e59 'held-out' = the other half of the same eval sequences (dCE unaffected). e05 'prominence' key is |c|/|x_raw| (mislabel). e02 'global' baseline targets block b's largest write (harder than the token's dominant write) so its 2-4x is target-specific.
- CAVEAT (inherited from the paper): the centering mean includes sink states: 4-8% constant offset in centered typical states in SmolLM2/Qwen/OLMo/Pythia (<1% GPT-2); slightly overstates the weight-vs-rotated gap and puts a constant sink atom in typical supports. e120 tests typical-only centering.
- Verified by the audit: hooks and ledger identity (ATT includes the output bias; acts = down-projection input; H[NB] raw), level conventions, row mappings, subsample alignment, disjoint centering slices, OMP/one-shot/refit indexing.
## New results (wave 5)
- e105 SPLICE (gpt2, 8 seqs; reconstruction replaces the level-6 state, rest of the model runs): base CE 3.577; OMP@64 dCE +0.18 (FVU 0.04 incl. sinks); one-shot@64 refit +1.61 (0.11); dual +1.66 (0.11); 64 largest TRUE writes refit +1.26 (0.09); increment pipeline +1.30 (0.09); random 64 atoms +5.9; mean only +6.2. FUNCTION TRACKS RECONSTRUCTION, NOT ATTRIBUTION: the best attributions cost 7x the cross-entropy of the best reconstruction, and the true largest writes are not a functional summary either.
- e77 targeted dictionaries (gpt2, full state, block-b atoms + embeddings only): recall of block-b-born writes 0.58-0.90 vs 0.28-0.72 with the full dictionary (MLP-only targeted 0.62-0.90; increment 0.48-0.90): cross-block atom competition costs 0.2-0.3 recall in GPT-2, as much as cancellation.
- e81 dictionary drift (pythia): MLP atom cosine with the final atoms 0.35 (4k) -> 0.61 (16k) -> 0.78 (32k) -> 0.93 (64k); final states read with the 64k dictionary: recall 0.46 vs 0.47, FVU 0.503 vs 0.500; write directions keep rotating until late in training but the last quarter is stable.
- e65 Wiener bound (gpt2, 200 dominant neurons): a ridge readout of the true coefficient from the centered state reaches out-of-sample R2 median 0.72 (q10 0.47; 88% above 0.5); never-identified neurons 0.69 vs always 0.75; Spearman(R2, recall) 0.07: the information is linearly present for nearly every neuron; identification failures are not information failures.
- e104 increments over other blocks' atoms (gpt2, k=16): own 0.49-0.60 < past 0.64-0.74 ~ future 0.63-0.73 < rotated 0.80: an increment is best explained by its own atoms; earlier and later atoms explain it equally (shared coordinate system).
- e93 IHT/HTP (gpt2): HTP r1 0.77 r3 0.34 err 0.29 FVU 0.53; IHT 0.77/0.34 but err 0.99: third solver family confirms identification up / reconstruction down.
- e80 wrong-model control (gpt2): trained states over the random-init GPT-2's dictionary: FVU32 0.500 = random atoms 0.500 = rotated 0.529 vs trained dictionary 0.338; random-init states over the trained dictionary 0.529. Provenance is the checkpoint's, not the architecture's.
- e58 gpt2_rand: predicted ~0 vs measured 0.28 (L0) -> 0.01: at init identification at L0 exceeds the prominence prediction (little competition), corr 0.99 overall.
- e11 v2 qwen: attention irrelevant (0.467 vs 0.461). e102 qwen: pruning 400 fillers hurts (0.463 -> 0.402); fillers diffuse in all models.
- e105 splice (all): OMP@64 dCE +0.18/+0.10/+0.13/+0.36 (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia); dual@64 +1.66/+0.74/+0.68/+1.43; one-shot@64 +1.61/+1.44/+0.84/+2.23; 64 largest TRUE writes refit +1.26/+1.25/+1.47/+2.82; increment pipeline +1.30/+2.74/+1.48/+3.54; random-64 +5.9/+7.7/+6.2/+6.8; mean only +6.2/+6.5/+6.4/+6.9. Function follows reconstruction, not attribution, in every model; the true largest writes are not a functional summary of the state.
- e77 targeted (smollm2/qwen/pythia): block-b writes from the full state with only block-b atoms: 0.12-0.97 vs full dictionary 0.02-0.96 vs increment 0.85-1.00. In the gated models and Pythia the increment reading dominates the targeted full-state reading (cancellation-limited); in GPT-2 targeting recovers most of the gap (competition-limited).
- e65 Wiener (all): linear R2 of a dominant neuron's coefficient from the state: median 0.72/0.56/0.51/0.62 (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia), never-identified 0.69/0.46/0.42/0.65 vs always 0.75/0.62/0.68/0.72; Spearman(R2, WDD recall) 0.07/0.16/0.43/0.06. Most writes are linearly readable, including those WDD never identifies.
- e76 (meta) recovered survival: once the atom drops out of the support (L2-L4 onward), WDD's own reading of a block-1 write's fate is 0 while the true projection survival is 2-5: the instrument reports 'gone' for amplified writes.
- e104 (all): an increment is best explained by its own atoms (0.43-0.72), and by earlier and later atoms equally (0.64-0.82) vs rotated 0.76-0.84: shared coordinate system, no directional composition asymmetry.
- e93 (all): HTP/IHT identify like LASSO (gpt2 0.77, qwen 0.60, smollm2 0.67) and reconstruct worse (FVU 0.44-0.53).
- e02 gpt2_rand: block-local recall@8 0.13-0.27 at init (trained 0.27-0.75); e11 v2 olmo: minus attention 0.632 vs 0.703 (attention mildly reinforcing).
- e105 olmo: OMP +0.42, dual +1.42, true-64 refit +1.91, pipeline +3.28, random +6.4. e77 olmo: full 0.14-0.99 / targeted 0.37-1.00 / increment 0.99-1.00. e65 olmo: R2 median 0.27 (likely sample-limited: 8k tokens for a 2048-d ridge; not comparable). e93 pythia: HTP 0.59/0.16 FVU 0.65. e76 qwen/pythia: recovered survival 0 once the atom drops out (L4+) while true survival stays 1.1-1.8.
- e118 anisotropy (gpt2): d_eff 270; max|cos| with the dictionary: isotropic random 0.156, covariance-matched random 0.300, real states 0.347; 64th-largest 0.117 / 0.191 / 0.202. The 'structured competition' is mostly the state covariance (effective dimension), a second-order property; the residual excess is small (0.30 -> 0.35 max, 0.19 -> 0.20 64th). The floor should be stated with d_eff.
- e104 olmo: own 0.47-0.78 < past = future 0.72-0.90 < rot 0.91; e93 olmo: HTP 0.78/0.39 FVU 0.61.
- e119 target rank (qwen/pythia): the 'most prominent true write' has ledger rank median 4/9 (q90 473/5232), is the largest write in 19%/18% of tokens, top-3 in 42%/35%, |c|/|c_max| median 0.55/0.53 (a mid-sized write); recall omp 0.92/0.65, dual 0.98/0.94. NON-CIRCULAR target = largest |c| among writes with raw survival > 0.75 (exists for 100% of tokens; is the largest write in 56%/78%): recall omp 0.58/0.55, dual 0.76/0.76, lasso 0.76/0.73. Honest ceiling: the largest physically surviving write is read at ~0.76 by the dual reading.
- e122 second OMP pass on the residual (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia): pass-1 real share 0.34/0.24/0.29/0.29 and 2.8/2.6/1.9/1.9 of the top-8 true writes found; pass 2 (64 more atoms): real share 0.11/0.08/0.05/0.17, new top-8 writes found 0.03/0.03/0.02/0.04 of 8, FVU 0.18 -> 0.055 (gpt2), 0.11 -> 0.02, 0.22 -> 0.07, 0.30 -> 0.12. Beyond the first support the decomposition is reconstruction only; attribution has stopped.

The cached mean's sink contamination puts the sink atom at the top of a third of SmolLM2's typical supports.

- e118 olmo: d_eff 630; max|cos| isotropic 0.101, covariance-matched 0.219, real 0.275; 64th 0.077/0.099/0.121: anisotropy explains most of the competitor level, with a larger residual excess than GPT-2.
- e126 olmo: sink atom b1#1710 (the published super-weight neuron) is the first pick in 60% of typical supports (|x_c . atom|/|x_c| 0.23); e122 olmo: pass-2 real share 0.52 -> 0.11, new top-8 0.10; e119 olmo: non-circular target (largest surviving write) recall omp 0.81 dual 0.94 lasso 0.93; most-prominent rank median 3.

- e119 gpt2/smollm2: non-circular target (largest surviving write; it is the largest write in 97%/92% of tokens): omp 0.64/0.70, dual 0.81/0.84, lasso 0.86/0.85. 'Most prominent' in SmolLM2 has ledger rank median 34 and |c|/|c_max| 0.23 (the circularity concern is real there). Honest non-circular ceiling across five models: dual 0.76-0.94.

The competitor level is the state covariance's 64th-order statistic; the prominence distribution then predicts identification at every depth of every model without bias.

## Second session (4-hour free exploration, started 02:17 EDT)
- e156 gradient attribution (gpt2, mid layer, 16 seqs): first-order loss attribution of write i = c_i (g . d_i). The gradient-important top-1 write is the ledger top-1 in 15% of tokens (top-3 overlap 0.22); WDD finds the gradient-important top-1 at 0.26 (OMP) / 0.60 (dual) vs the ledger top-1 at 0.67 / 0.81; among identified real writes Spearman(recovered |c|, |attribution|) +0.32 (true |c|: +0.59); prominence vs gradient sensitivity +0.03; the share of total |loss attribution| carried by WDD's real atoms is 0.01. What the loss is sensitive to and what WDD reads are nearly disjoint at the token level.
- e155 confidence (gpt2): Spearman(prominence, next-token entropy) +0.23; recall on low/high-entropy quartiles 0.60 / 0.72; AUC(low entropy -> identified) 0.44. Writes are MORE readable when the model is uncertain.
- e146 token vs context (pythia): level 1: prominence R2 by token id 0.95, same token -> same dominant neuron 1.00; mid layer: 0.25 / 0.39; last level: 0.20 / 0.70. Early = token lookup, middle = context, output = token again.
- e145 head subspaces (pythia, data-free): MLP atoms' energy in the best single head subspace 0.07-0.14 (any block) vs 0.08 for random rank-64 subspaces; >0.5 in 0-2%: MLP writes do not live inside single head subspaces (the late-level aliasing partners are special cases).
- e141 eraser (olmo, born block 1): 48% of the larger half end erased; the largest survival drop is at L2 (49%) / L3 (27%); the top-1 opposing neuron carries 0.00 of the cancellation, top-5 0.01: cancellation is diffuse; opposing and reinforcing crowds are each ~16x the write (the massive-channel direction).
- e147 INCREMENT PHENOTYPE: state-level never-neurons are readable in their own block's increment at 1.00 (SmolLM2), 0.98 (Qwen), 0.91 (Pythia), 0.93 (OLMo) with mean increment recall 0.89-0.99; in GPT-2 only 0.11 (mean 0.20). The never-class is a cross-block phenomenon in four models and intrinsic (within-block) in GPT-2. Increment-level recall 0.83/1.00/0.98/0.95/1.00; increment ICC 0.97/0.56/0.88/0.91/0.98.
- e131 PROVENANCE SURVIVAL CURVE (dual reading; top-1 / top-3 / top-8 true writes): write level 1.00 everywhere -> increment 0.85/0.77/0.67 (gpt2), 1.00/0.99/0.97 (smollm2), 0.98/0.97/0.94 (qwen), 0.97/0.95/0.90 (pythia) -> state 0.80/0.68/0.53, 0.82/0.66/0.47, 0.62/0.53/0.40, 0.67/0.55/0.41. Energy of the top-8 recovered: write 1.00 -> increment 0.74/0.99/0.97/0.92 -> state 0.63/0.79/0.51/0.50. Provenance is lost at the accumulation step, not at the write -> increment step (GPT-2 loses 15-33% within the block).
- e130 phenotype: within-neuron AUC(prominence -> hit) 0.72-0.82; between-neuron R2 of recall: mean prominence 0.58/0.51/0.64/0.74/0.44, geometry only 0.42/0.05/0.02/0.08/0.37 (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia/olmo), both 0.65-0.75; matched-|c| same-neuron pairs: ties 0.72-0.96 (both identified or both not); between-neuron sd of recall 0.35-0.41 -> 0.17-0.24 after conditioning on token prominence. 'Neuron property' = mostly the neuron's typical prominence (an amplitude phenotype), plus geometry in GPT-2 and OLMo.
- e129 HELD-OUT LAW: competitor level from the odd sequences, predicting even: vs one-shot corr 0.97/0.99/0.97 MAE 0.045/0.022/0.033 bias +0.03/0.00/0.00 (gpt2/smollm2/pythia); per-token rule 'identified iff prominence > level' accuracy 0.93-0.94 (one-shot), 0.90-0.91 (dual), 0.77-0.88 (OMP); step-64k covariance predicting step-143k: corr 0.94 MAE 0.057 bias +0.04. Zero parameters, held out: the observability law survives.
- e132 attention -> MLP -> WDD chain (gpt2, 24 heads of blocks 4-5): a head ablation changes the level-6 dominant write for ~3% of tokens per head; the change is fully visible in the state along the write's direction (corr 0.83, slope 1.06); WDD tracks it with the right slope under OMP (slope 1.03, corr 0.36) and attenuated under the dual reading (slope 0.61, corr 0.49), for the ~0.6 of tokens identified before and after. Attention-mediated changes of downstream writes are recoverable.
- e140 all runs OOMed (per-token gather of [NT, DFF, D]); patched to per-block computation and re-queued.
- e152 functional split (gpt2, 8 seqs): removing the part of the state explained by WDD's identified REAL atoms (13.5 atoms/token, 36% of energy) costs +2.48 nats; the spurious part (48% of energy) +1.79; the whole reconstruction +5.40; the true top-3 writes exactly (7% of energy) +0.16; energy-matched random true writes +0.22. Function scales with energy removed; the identified provenance is collectively more functional per unit energy than the spurious part, while single-write first-order attributions (e156) are tiny: importance is collective and nonlinear, not per-write.
- e144 guided OMP (candidates = increment-nominated atoms + embeddings): qwen real share 0.29 -> 0.91, r1 0.47 -> 0.55, FVU 0.216 -> 0.261, dCE +0.127 -> +0.189; pythia real 0.30 -> 0.86, FVU 0.30 -> 0.37, dCE +0.36 -> +0.53. Best compromise decoder so far.
- e157 last level (gpt2, tied): the predicted next token's atom is in the k=64 support 39%; the first token atom picked is the prediction 31%, in the top-5 57%.
- e153 toy superposition (Elhage-style ReLU toy): light superposition (n400 d40): recall omp 0.79, one-shot 0.93, dual 0.92, law prediction 0.97; readability vs feature importance rho +0.26/+0.38; heavy (n1600 d40): omp 0.46 > one-shot 0.29 < dual 0.59, law 0.25 (one-shot 0.29): the law transfers to the toy, and the decoder order flips under heavy superposition (peelable competitors).
- e146 olmo: L1 R2(token) 0.53 / modal 0.78; mid 0.20 / 0.37; last 0.19 / 0.23. e156 smollm2: share of loss attribution in WDD's real atoms 0.01; gradient-important = ledger top-1 in 32%.
- e140 consumption vs broadcast (read strength of a write's direction by the next block's MLP input rows, relative to a random direction): GPT-2 dominant writes 1.22x; AUC(strongly read -> erased) 0.72 in GPT-2 but 0.32 (SmolLM2) / 0.37 (Pythia): in GPT-2 the erased writes are the consumed ones, in the gated models the erased writes are the LESS-read ones (discarded). Neuron level: recall vs read strength -0.11 to -0.18 everywhere (more-read neurons slightly less readable). Gate rows (SmolLM2): AUC(low read -> identified) 0.66.
- e152 smollm2 functional split: removing the identified-real part (40% energy) +4.93 nats vs the spurious part (56%) +1.39: the identified provenance is 3.5x more costly to remove, 5.0x per unit energy (corrected when the atlas was built: 3.5x is the raw ratio); removing the true top-3 writes exactly (7% energy) +2.72 vs energy-matched random true writes +2.57. In SmolLM2 the largest writes are functional (token-lookup writes feed tied-embedding prediction); in GPT-2 they were not (+0.16).
- e129 qwen: held-out corr 0.99 (dual 1.00), rule accuracy 0.96. e131 olmo: 1.00 -> 1.00/0.99/0.92 -> 0.83/0.74/0.59. e144 olmo: real share 0.52 -> 0.93 (FVU 0.43 -> 0.51, dCE +0.42 -> +0.72). e157 smollm2: prediction atom in support 0.15 (the last state points away from the current token).
- e161 why uncertain tokens are readable (gpt2): entropy vs centered state norm -0.38, vs |c| +0.15, vs birth block -0.15; low vs high entropy quartile: state norm 71 vs 61, block-0-born dominant writes 43% vs 67%, prominence 0.25 vs 0.31. High-entropy tokens have smaller states dominated by the token-lookup write.
- e154 reweighted l1 (gpt2): LASSO r1 0.85 raw ratio 0.29 -> after one reweighting round ratio 0.57, refit err 0.28, r1 0.85, FVU 0.43: reweighting halves the shrinkage, identification and reconstruction unchanged.
- e156 qwen: share of loss attribution in WDD's real atoms 0.00; gradient top-1 = ledger top-1 27%. e140 olmo: no read-strength relation (AUC ~0.5).
- e143 increment + OV value atoms (gpt2, blocks 3-6): attention energy captured within the increment 0.08-0.15 (static atoms) -> 0.53-0.56 (value atoms); increment FVU16 0.56-0.59 -> 0.49-0.51; MLP recall unchanged. Half of a block's attention write becomes attributable once attention has its proper (context-dependent) atoms.
- e162 differential WDD (pairs of occurrences of the same token): the largest differential write is read from the state DIFFERENCE at 0.33/0.43 (omp/dual, gpt2) and 0.36/0.43 (smollm2) vs 0.17/0.35 and 0.19/0.25 from a single state; its prominence doubles (0.13 -> 0.25, 0.09 -> 0.15); in SmolLM2 the differential writes are all late-born (block-0 share 0.00). Subtracting the shared token-lookup part reads context.
- e158 MDL (gpt2, 8 bits/coefficient, log2 m = 16.3 bits/index): FVU <= 0.5: weight 16 atoms = 388 bits vs PCA 64 components = 512 bits; FVU <= 0.3: 48 atoms = 1164 bits vs PCA 192 = 1536 bits; FVU <= 0.15: 96 atoms vs PCA > 256. The model's own dictionary is a ~25% shorter code than PCA at equal distortion despite paying the index cost.
- e151 support as code (smollm2): kNN-10 next-token accuracy from state cosine 0.273, from the dual support's Jaccard overlap 0.264, OMP support 0.239, coefficient cosine 0.225, random supports 0.021, majority 0.051; 1-NN agreement with the state geometry 0.32-0.49. The 64-atom support is a near-lossless hash of the state's predictive geometry.
- e155 pythia: no entropy relation (recall 0.51/0.52 by quartile). e161 smollm2: entropy vs state norm -0.31, birth-0 share 0.26 -> 0.41: same mechanism as GPT-2. e156 pythia: share of loss attribution 0.00. e157 qwen: prediction atom in the support 0.27.
- e152 pythia: identified-real part (18% energy) +1.26 nats vs spurious (53%) +1.45: real part 2.6x more functional per unit energy (corrected from 2.4x when the atlas was built); true top-3 writes +0.06 (5% energy) vs matched random +0.03. Pattern across models: the identified provenance is collectively more functional per unit energy than the spurious part (per unit energy 1.9x GPT-2, 2.6x Pythia, 5.0x SmolLM2; the first version gave 1.4-3.5x, which mixed raw and per-energy ratios); the largest writes are functional in SmolLM2 only.
- e157 pythia (untied, unembedding rows appended): prediction atom in the support 0.45, first token atom = prediction 0.20.
- e159 guided decoder front (nominations per block 4/8/16/32 + embeddings; OMP@64 on the state): GPT-2 global real 0.34 / r1 0.68 / FVU 0.178 / dCE +0.18 -> guided_32 real 0.76 / 0.71 / 0.221 / +0.33 (guided_8: real 0.85, FVU 0.238, dCE +0.43); SmolLM2 global 0.25 / 0.67 / 0.114 / +0.10 -> guided_32 0.74 / 0.76 / 0.163 / +0.17. Without embedding atoms: r1 0.81/0.88, r3 0.34/0.53 but FVU 0.37/0.32 and dCE +0.84/+0.46. Most of the attribution gain costs little function.
- e158 mdl smollm2: at FVU 0.5/0.3/0.15 the weight code is 389/779/1558 bits vs PCA 256/768/1536: equal to PCA (low-rank states, d_eff 118); GPT-2's weight code was ~25% shorter than PCA. Model-dependent.
- e162 qwen: differential reading 0.14/0.17 vs 0.10/0.17 (weak). e129 olmo: held-out corr 0.99, dual 1.00, rule accuracy 0.96-0.97. e154 smollm2: reweighting does not change identification (0.83) or FVU (0.28); raw ratio 0.24 -> 0.30. e161 qwen: entropy vs norm -0.35, birth-0 share 0.10 -> 0.23.
- e167 predicted survival curve (gpt2, zero parameters): state level top-1/3/8 predicted 0.79/0.67/0.53 vs measured 0.80/0.68/0.53 (exact); increment level predicted 0.70/0.60/0.52 vs measured 0.85/0.77/0.67 (the 16th-order block level over-estimates the competition inside GPT-2's coherent blocks).
- e152 olmo: identified-real part (31% energy) +2.81 nats vs the spurious part (24% energy) +0.20: in OLMo the spurious atoms carry almost no function (11x less per unit energy, 14x in raw cost; corrected when the atlas was built); true top-3 +0.72 vs matched random +1.00.
- e155 olmo: the entropy relation reverses (recall 0.80 on low-entropy vs 0.61 on high-entropy tokens; AUC 0.59): the uncertainty-readability link is model-specific (GPT-2/SmolLM2 positive, Pythia none, OLMo negative).
- e160 qwen per-write ablation: per identified real write, dCE median +0.005, mean +0.089, negative (helpful to remove) in 43%; Spearman(ablation, first-order attribution) +0.46; the first real atom carries 0.55 of the total |effect| and one atom dominates in 53% of tokens.
- e151 qwen: dual-support Jaccard kNN 0.269 vs state cosine 0.280 (random 0.021). e156 olmo: share of loss attribution 0.01; gradient top-1 = ledger top-1 24%.
- e166 stream reconstruction (gpt2; sum of per-block increment OMP reconstructions + exact embedding, spliced): k=8/block (56 atoms) +2.03 nats, k=16 (112) +0.96, k=32 (224) +0.44 vs global OMP@64 +0.18 (embedding only +9.6). The increment reading preserves function only with 3-4x the atom budget: provenance (increments) and function (state) are different objects at equal budget.
- e168 peeling the early increments (gpt2): reading later-block writes after subtracting H[1]-H[0] (or H[2]-H[0]) does not help (OMP 0.54 -> 0.47/0.49; dual 0.64 -> 0.69 / 0.71 -> 0.65): the early increments are not what obscures later writes; the competition is among later atoms.
- e167 smollm2: increment-level predictions 0.99/0.96/0.90 vs measured 1.00/0.99/0.97; state-level 0.69/0.50/0.32 vs measured (dual) 0.82/0.66/0.47: the dual reading beats the raw-projection law at the state level (whitening changes the competition).
- e171 TWO-LAYER TOY (negative for the 'accumulation alone decouples' hypothesis): block-1 width 0/64/256: recall 0.91/0.86/0.83, survival ~1.0-1.1, readability vs importance rho +0.22/+0.34/+0.40 and prominence vs importance +0.21/+0.39/+0.55 (readable = important in the toy); width 1024: recall collapses to 0.04 (block-1 writes swamp the stream; law prediction 0.07 matches) and rho(prom, imp) -0.10. Accumulation by one extra block does not reproduce the real models' decoupling; that needs their multi-block redundancy and cancellation.
- e162 pythia: differential 0.25/0.37 vs single 0.15/0.21 (prominence 0.06 -> 0.11); e161 pythia: entropy vs state norm -0.52, nothing else.
- e167 qwen: state top-1/3/8 predicted 0.64/0.55/0.41 vs measured 0.62/0.53/0.40; increment 0.99/0.98/0.95 vs 0.98/0.97/0.94: the provenance-survival curve is predicted with zero parameters (GPT-2 and Qwen exact; SmolLM2 under-predicts the dual reading at the state level).
- e166 smollm2: stream reconstruction k=8/16/32 per block: +1.42/+0.86/+0.46 nats (128/256/512 atoms) vs global OMP@64 +0.10. e168 smollm2: peeling early increments does not help (0.50 -> 0.48). e160 pythia: per-write ablation Spearman with first-order attribution +0.56; negative in 45%; first real atom carries 0.38 of the effect. e157 olmo (untied): prediction atom in support 0.46.
- e150 hook-free attention patterns (gpt2): joint NNLS over ALL heads' value atoms on the block's attention write recovers patterns at corr 0.18-0.31 (top position 0.17-0.32), and on the raw increment 0.08-0.18. Attention patterns are recoverable per head from that head's own write (e53: 0.5-0.85), not jointly from the block's attention write or hook-free from the increment. Negative.
- e174 redundancy toy (twin re-writes at gain 0.5-2): block-0 atom recall stays 0.85 (both the original and its twin get picked: 'either' 0.88), survival 1.05-1.58, rho(readable, importance) +0.22 to +0.35: no decoupling. Neither one extra block nor twin redundancy reproduces the real models' readable-vs-important independence; it is a many-block, many-write phenomenon. Negative (kept).
- e159 qwen: guided_4 real 0.95 (from 0.29), r1 0.56, FVU 0.266, dCE +0.19. e166 qwen: stream reconstruction k=32/block (416 atoms) +0.80 nats, FVU 0.70 (independent per-block reconstructions accumulate error). e167 pythia: state 0.63/0.51/0.37 pred vs 0.67/0.55/0.41; increment 0.93/0.90/0.82 vs 0.97/0.95/0.90. e168 qwen: peeling the early increments HURTS (0.38 -> 0.29; dual 0.52 -> 0.42): in Qwen the early increments reinforce later writes (as attention does in Pythia).
- e162 olmo: differential 0.50/0.54 vs single 0.36/0.41. e168 pythia: peeling hurts (0.44 -> 0.34; dual 0.60 -> 0.48): early increments reinforce later writes in Pythia and Qwen; neutral in GPT-2/SmolLM2. e159 pythia: guided_32 real 0.84 (from 0.30), r1 0.59, dCE +0.51 (from +0.36). e166 pythia: summed increment reconstructions have FVU > 1 (errors accumulate); the increment reading is not a stream reconstruction. e158 qwen: weight code LONGER than PCA (825 vs 384 bits at FVU 0.5; 2476 vs 2048 at 0.15): the MDL advantage of the model's dictionary is GPT-2-specific (equal in SmolLM2, worse in Qwen). e161 olmo: no entropy relation.
- e170 off-channel (gpt2/smollm2): projecting out the 5 massive channels (12-14% of typical variance): GPT-2 increment recall 0.56 -> 0.56, state OMP 0.64 -> 0.65 (dual 0.80 -> 0.86); SmolLM2 0.81 -> 0.81, 0.68 -> 0.70. GPT-2's within-block unreadability is NOT the massive-channel dynamics (hypothesis killed); it is ordinary coherence + within-block cancellation.
- e175 MANY-BLOCK TOY (positive): B=1: recall 0.80/0.85, survival 1.09, cancel index 0.59, rho(prom, imp) +0.61; B=4: recall 0.05/0.15 (law 0.05), survival 2.42 (amplified), erased 26%, cancel index 2.17, rho(prom, imp) -0.06; B=8: 0.03/0.12 (law 0.03), survival 2.15, erased 35%, cancel 2.97, rho(prom, imp) -0.05. Four or more residual blocks reproduce the real models' phenomenology: amplification with unreadability, reinforcing block writes, the law, and the decoupling of prominence from importance. Depth (many blocks writing along the same directions) is the mechanism; one extra block or twin redundancy was not enough.
- e167 olmo: state 0.84/0.76/0.63 pred vs 0.83/0.74/0.59; increment 1.00/0.98/0.89 vs 1.00/0.99/0.92 (exact). e151 pythia: support code 0.237/0.220 vs state 0.258. e154 qwen: reweighting raises the LASSO ratio 0.09 -> 0.21, identification unchanged (0.62). e160 olmo: Spearman(ablation, attribution) +0.49, negative in 45%.
- e176 toy survival curve (6 blocks): write 1.00 -> increment 0.88 -> state 0.11 (dual) / 0.04 (OMP); the feature's write survival is 2.53 (amplified); for state-level misses the closest support atom has cosine 0.26 with the feature direction (none above 0.5): NOT near-twin aliasing; the later blocks dominate the state's norm along their own directions so the feature's prominence falls below the level (swamping). rho(increment readability, importance) +0.29; rho(survival, importance) -0.11.
- e169 code budget: kNN next-token accuracy from support Jaccard at k=4/8/16/32/64: gpt2 0.184/0.218/0.231/0.238/0.244 (state 0.258), smollm2 0.206/0.228/0.242/0.244/0.239 (state 0.273); the increment-union code (8 atoms per block) 0.249 / 0.266 is the best sparse code, within 0.01 of the state.
- e158 pythia mdl: weight 795 bits vs PCA 768 at FVU 0.5; PCA cannot reach FVU 0.3 within 256 components (d_eff 322) while the weight code does with 96 atoms: high-d_eff models (GPT-2, Pythia) favour the model's dictionary, low-d_eff ones (SmolLM2, Qwen) favour PCA.
- e170 pythia/qwen: off-channel unchanged (0.62 -> 0.62, 0.83 -> 0.83): universal. e166 olmo: stream reconstruction k=32 +0.86 vs global +0.42. e151 olmo: code 0.244 vs state 0.252. e168 olmo: peeling hurts (0.70 -> 0.64).
- e160 per-write ablation (gpt2/smollm2/qwen/pythia/olmo): Spearman with first-order attribution +0.40/+0.42/+0.46/+0.56/+0.49; removals that HELP 41/39/43/45/45%; the first real atom carries 0.25/0.70/0.55/0.38/0.45 of the total |effect| and one atom dominates in 36/66/53/48/31% of tokens (SmolLM2's token-lookup write dominates).
- e159 olmo: guided_32 real 0.91 (from 0.52), r1 0.76, dCE +0.68 (from +0.42); without embeddings r1 0.94, r3 0.69, real 0.83 (FVU 0.59, dCE +1.5). e154 pythia: reweighting raises the ratio 0.40 -> 0.81 at r1 0.60. e170 olmo: off-channel unchanged.
- e177 toy guided decoding (6 blocks): block-0 feature at the state level: full omp 0.04 / dual 0.11 -> increment-guided 0.36 / 0.34; block-0 atoms only 0.13 / 0.22; later-block share of state energy 0.39. The guided decoder triples the state-level reading in the toy but does not reach the increment level (0.88): once accumulated, part of the provenance is gone from the state for any decoder.
- e158 olmo mdl: weight 1206 bits vs PCA 2048 at FVU 0.5 (d_eff 630); at FVU 0.3/0.15 PCA cannot reach within 256 components. MDL verdict: the model's dictionary is the shorter code where states are high-rank (GPT-2, Pythia, OLMo), equal or longer where they are low-rank (SmolLM2, Qwen).
- e173 ERASURE MATRIX (mean contribution of block b' to the raw survival of dominant writes born in block b, mid layer): GPT-2: net later contribution +1.1 to +1.7 for every birth block; the strongest canceller of a block-b write is block b itself (b1 -0.64, b2 -0.67, b3 -0.57: within-block); the strongest reinforcer is always block 0 (+0.5 to +1.2). SmolLM2: block 0 reinforces most birth blocks (+0.2 to +0.6); weak next-block cancellers (-0.15 to -0.34). Qwen: a NEXT-BLOCK canceller chain (b -> b+1: -0.33 to -0.44 for b = 3..10), net negative for blocks 1-8. Pythia: block 5 is a dedicated canceller of everything born in blocks 0-4 (-0.38 to -0.75), then a next-block chain (-0.4); block 0 reinforces (+2.3 own, +1.5 for block-1 writes). OLMo: next-block canceller chain throughout (-0.33 to -0.43), net negative for blocks 0-5. Two cancellation topologies: within-block (GPT-2) vs next-block chain (Qwen, OLMo, Pythia after block 5), plus block-0 directions being reinforced by later writes in GPT-2/SmolLM2/Pythia.
- e154 olmo: reweighting raises the ratio 0.34 -> 0.52 at r1 0.81. e172: certificate tables written (gpt2 132 neurons, smollm2 123).
- e173 split (MLP / attention): the next-block cancellation in Qwen, OLMo and Pythia (after block 5) is carried by the next block's MLP (-0.2 to -0.4), with attention near zero; the GPT-2 within-block cancellation is the block's own other neurons (MLP -0.5 to -0.7) while GPT-2 attention reinforces everything (att total +7.5 vs MLP +3.0; block-0 attention already lies along the direction block-0 MLP is about to write: +0.66). The block-0 reinforcement of later writes is MLP-borne in SmolLM2/Qwen and mixed in GPT-2/Pythia.
- e183 qwen (CAUSAL TEST OF THE ERASER CHAIN, refutation of the naive reading): ablating the next block's MLP does NOT restore the cancelled writes. Born b2: survival 0.66 -> 0.61 (control = ablate block b+3: 0.60); b3: 0.79 -> 0.76 (0.74); b4: 0.90 -> 0.84 (0.84); recall unchanged. Born b1: survival 0.96 -> 0.68 (ablating block 2 hurts). So the erasure matrix is an additive DIRECT-effect decomposition; the total effect of removing the canceller is ~0 because later blocks compensate (hydra-style). Erasure looks like a role the next available block performs, not a wired pair -> e186 tests this directly.
- e183 all five (CAUSAL): ablating the next block's MLP never restores survival (Qwen/OLMo/Pythia/SmolLM2/GPT-2 all within +-0.05 of the ablate-b+3 control); identification barely moves, except OLMo where removing ANY MLP block raises recall of earlier writes (b3 OMP 0.36 -> 0.58 next-block vs 0.44 control) = fewer competitors = less swamping, and the next block is the strongest competitor. Direct effect != total effect for erasure.
- e186 HYDRA ON THE ERASER: when block b+1's MLP is ablated, the remaining later blocks take over its cancellation. OLMo: sum of MLP contributions from blocks >= b+2 goes -0.03 -> -0.36, -0.17 -> -0.59, -0.24 -> -0.53, -0.22 -> -0.53 (the removed next-block term was -0.27 to -0.31); Pythia b4: removed -0.74, later sum -1.22 -> -1.83; b1: removed a +0.50 reinforcer, later cancellation weakens -2.15 -> -1.79. Attention+embedding ('rest') shifts the other way by +0.2 to +0.6. GPT-2: b+2's direct term flips from +0.08 to -0.1/-0.27. Erasure is a feedback role performed by whichever later blocks see the direction still present, not a wired pair.
- e191 LINEARITY: relative cancellation by the main canceller is constant across write-magnitude quartiles in Qwen (-0.28/-0.29/-0.30/-0.30), OLMo (-0.29/-0.31/-0.30/-0.28) and GPT-2 within-block (-0.58/-0.54/-0.51/-0.53): proportional (gain-like) damping. Pythia: relative falls with magnitude (-0.27 -> -0.10) while the ABSOLUTE cancellation is roughly constant (-0.47, -0.47, -0.42, -0.31): an additive offset, bias-like (e192 tests the MLP output bias). SmolLM2: next-block term tiny (-0.05) but the net later cancellation grows with magnitude (-0.22 -> -0.80): saturating, large writes are trimmed more.
- e187 STATIC (weights only): anti-aligned partner rows (cos < -0.5) exist mainly WITHIN a block and mostly in GPT-2 (6.1% of rows within, 3.0% next block, 1.1% three blocks later, 0.0% random); Qwen 2.1/0.8/0.3, OLMo 1.2/0.5/0.1, SmolLM2 1.1/0.7/0.3, Pythia 0.5/0.1/0.0. GPT-2's within-block cancellation is partly wired into the weights; the next-block chains have almost no static signature (Pythia, a GELU model where sign is meaningful: 0.1%), so they are activation-driven crowds, consistent with e186.
- e188 SURVIVAL vs DEPTH (raw): Qwen: drops concentrated in the first 2-4 blocks after the write (median drop +1: 0.31, +2: 0.16, later ~0.01), then flat, then a late-layer RE-AMPLIFICATION of early write directions (born-b1 survival 1.13 at mid depth -> 2.27 near the top, born-b0 1.4 -> 2.75). OLMo: smooth decay (1.40 -> 1.10 -> 0.87 -> 0.73 -> 0.63 -> 0.57 -> ... 0.38), between one-step and exponential. Pythia: no early drop; slow decline after block 5 (b0: 3.2 -> 3.7 at +2 -> 1.0 at the top). GPT-2 and SmolLM2: monotone GROWTH of survival with depth (GPT-2 born-b3: 2.8 -> 12.8; SmolLM2 born-b0: 1.4 -> 3.8, then a drop in the last 4 levels). Raw survival conflates persistence of the write with growth of the state along common directions -> e193 repeats on centered states.
- e181 MISS ANATOMY (real models): at mid depth, misses are swamped, not aliased: the support atom closest to the write direction has cos > 0.5 in only 1-2% of misses for Qwen/OLMo/Pythia/SmolLM2, but 47% for GPT-2 (GPT-2 is the aliasing model, as in e41). Misses have lower survival than hits (Qwen 1.03 vs 2.19, OLMo 0.49 vs 0.85, Pythia 0.95 vs 1.58). At the late layers recall is 0 and the state norm is 3-15x: everything is swamped.
